# Tzava'at HaRivash

צוואת הריב"ש

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash)

אוסף דרשותיו של הבעל שם טוב וממשיך דרכו המגיד ממזעריטש, מוצג כצוואה מוסרית.

Collection of teachings of the Ba'al Shem Tov and his successor, the Maggid of Mezeritch, presented as an ethical will.

Sections: Chapter-Verse

Location: Chasidut/Early Works

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): Warsaw, 1913 (Segments: 312, License: Public Domain)

Version (En): The Way of The Baal Shem Tov. Adapted into English by Rabbi Amiram Markel and Yehudah Shimon Markel, 2020 (Segments: 312)


## Chapter 1

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/1:1)


**צוואת הריב"ש ע"ה.** להיות תמים בעבודתו יתברך עבודה תמה ועיקר שלא לשכוח הדברים. ועיקר ללמוד בכל יום שיעור מוסר הן רב הן מעט.


Rabbi Yisrael Baal Shem, peace be upon him, taught us to be wholehearted in our service of *HaShem*, blessed is He and to serve Him with simplicity.¹ Of primary importance is not to forget the Commandments we received directly from *HaShem*at *Chorev*² (*Aseret HaDvarim-עשרת הדברים*),³, your God, at *Chorev* etc.” and to study teachings of *Mussar*, that deal with developing good character traits and right conduct, on a daily basis, whether one studies a little or a lot.

footnotes:
¹ Deuteronomy 18:13 and Rashi there.
² Mount Sinai
³ Deuteronomy 4:9-10 – “Only beware for yourself and greatly beware for your soul, lest you forget the things that your eyes have beheld and lest you remove them from your heart all the days of your life, and make them known to your children and your children’s children – the day that you stood before HaShem


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/1:2)


ולראות א"ע תמיד להדבק א"ע במדות טובות והנהגות ישרות ושלא יניח שום יום מעשיית מצוה הן קלה הן חמורה. וסימנך הוי זהיר במצוה קלה כבחמורה פירש זהיר מלשון והמשכילים יזהירו. ר"ל הנשמה תזהיר ותאיר ממצוה קלה כבחמורה כי רחמנא ליבא בעי:


Make sure to always adhere to good character traits and right conduct and do not let a day go by without doing a *mitzvah*-commandment, be it a minor *mitzvah* or a major one. The mnemonic by which to remember this is the verse,⁴ “Be as radiant-*Zahir*-*זהיר* from a minor *mitzvah* as from a major one.” The word *Zahir*-*זהיר,*which is usually translated as “careful,” is related, in its root, to the terminology of the verse,⁵ “And they that are wise will be radiant-*Yazhiru*-*יזהירו*as the radiance-*Zohar-זהר*of the firmament.” This is to say that the soul should be as radiant and illuminated by the performance of a minor *mitzvah* as from a major one, for “the Merciful One desires the heart.”⁶

footnotes:
⁴ Mishnah Avot 2:1
⁵ Daniel 12:3
⁶ Zohar Vol. 2, 162b; Talmud Bavli, Sanhedrin 106b.



## Chapter 2

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/2:1)


**שויתי ה' לנגדי תמיד.** שויתי לשון השתוות בכל דבר המאורע הכל שוה אצלו בין בענין שמשבחין ב"א אותו או מבזין אותו. וכן בכל שאר דברי' וכן בכל המאכלות בין שאוכל מעדנים בין שאוכל שאר דברי' הכל ישוה בעיניו כיון שהוסר היצה"ר ממנו מכל וכל.


The Psalmist states,⁷ “I have set *HaShem* before me always.” The term, “I have set-*Shiviti*-*שויתי*,” is of the same root as the term, “equanimity-*Hishtavut*-*השתוות*.” That is, everything that happens should be equal to you, whether people praise or shame you. This applies to all other matters as well. Likewise, all foods, whether they are delicacies or other things, should all be equal in your eyes. This matter applies if you have removed the evil inclination completely from yourself.

footnotes:
⁷ Psalms 16:8


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/2:2)


וכל דבר שיארע לו יאמר הלא זה הוא מאתו יתברך ואם בעיניו הגון וכו' וכל כוונתו לש"ש אבל מצד עצמו אין חילוק וזה מדרגה גדולה מאד.


Whatever happens to you, say to yourself, “Is this not from *HaShem*, blessed is He? If it is proper in His eyes, it certainly is proper in mine.” That is, your motivation should be entirely for the sake of *HaShem*, blessed is He, but from your own perspective, it makes no difference. This is a very lofty level.⁸-שויתי” of the year 5720 by the Lubavitcher Rebbe, as well as Tanya, Igeret HaKodesh, Epistle 11.

footnotes:
⁸ See the Chassidic discourse entitled “Shiviti



## Chapter 3

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/3:1)


וכל האדם צריך לעבוד השי"ת בכל כחו שהכל הוא צורך מפני שהשי"ת רוצה שיעבדו אותו בכל האופנים.


Every person must serve *HaShem*, blessed is He, with all his strength and all his aspects. For, *HaShem*desires that we serve Him with all aspects of our being, in every manner, for it all has profound purpose.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/3:2)


והכוונה כי לפעמים אדם הולך ומדבר עם ב"א ואז אינו יכול ללמוד וצריך להיות דבוק בהש"י וליחד יחודים. וכן כשהאדם הולך בדרך ואינו יכול להתפלל וללמוד כדרכו וצריך לעבוד אותו באופנים אחרים.


The meaning is that sometimes you may need travel or speak with others. At such times, you cannot study *HaShem’s* Torah. Nonetheless, you must adhere to *HaShem*, blessed is He, in thought, and unify yourself to Him. (This is called *Yichudim*). Thus, if you are travelling and are unable to pray or study as you are accustomed, you must serve *HaShem* in other ways.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/3:3)


ואל יצער את עצמו בזה כי השי"ת רוצה בזה שיעבדוהו בכל האופנים פעמי' באופן זה ופעמים באופן זה לכך הזדמן לפניו לילך לדרך או לדבר עם ב"א בכדי לעבוד אותו באופן הזה.


Moreover, do not become upset by this, for *HaShem*, blessed is He, desires to be served in all manner of service, at times in one way and at times in another way. *HaShem* therefore arranged that you would need to travel or speak with others, so that you could serve Him in a different way.



## Chapter 4

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/4:1)


כלל גדול גול על ה' מעשיך ויכונו מחשבותיך שכל דבר שיזדמן יחשוב שהוא מאתו יתברך ויראה שיבקש מהשי"ת שיזמין לו תמיד מה שהשי"ת יודע שהוא לטובתו ולא מה שנראה לב"א ע"פ שכלו כי אפשר מה שבעיניו טוב הוא רע לו רק ישליך הכל כל עניניו וצרכיו עליו יתברך:


An important principle is as follows,⁹ “Turn your deeds toward *HaShem*, and your thoughts will be set aright.” In other words, know that everything that comes about is from *HaShem*, blessed is He. See to it, to always request of *HaShem*, blessed is He, to bring about what He knows is for your good, rather than what appears to be good according to human intellect. For it is possible that what you think is good for you is actually bad. Instead, cast all your affairs and needs upon *HaShem*, blessed is He, as the verse states,¹⁰ “Cast your burden upon *HaShem* and He will sustain you.”

footnotes:
⁹ Proverbs 16:3
¹⁰ Psalms 55:23



## Chapter 5

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/5:1)


**ידבק מחשבתו למעלה** ולא יאכל ולא ישתה יותר מדאי ולא יענג עצמו רק כדי (רק שישתדל בכל ענינם) להבריא עצמו. ולא יסתכל כלל בעניני עוה"ז ולא יחשוב בהם כלל. כדי להפריד את עצמו מהגשמיות כי בהסתכלותו בעוה"ז מגשם עצמו. וארז"ל ראי' מביאה לידי זכירה ולידי תאוה. וכתיב בעץ הדעת נחמד למראה וטוב למאכל שמחמת הראי' הוא נחמד.


Bond your thoughts to *HaShem* above. Do not eat or drink excessively, nor eat for pleasure, but solely to maintain your health.¹¹ Do not look into the matters of this world at all, nor think about them altogether.¹² On the contrary, in all things endeavor to separate yourself from physicality. For by gazing at the matters of this world you become gross and spiritually callous, as our sages, of blessed memory, said,¹³ “Seeing leads to remembering, which leads to lust,” as written about the Tree of Knowledge, that it was,¹⁴ “Pleasing to the sight and good to eat,” that is, its appearance is what made it desirable.

footnotes:
¹¹ Mishneh Torah, Hilchot De’ot, 3:2 & Ch. 4.
¹² Mishneh Torah, Hilchot Yesodei HaTorah 7:1; Hilchot Teshuvah 10:6
¹³ Talmud Bavli Menachot 53b; Midrash Tanchuma 15; Rashi Numbers 15:39.
¹⁴ Genesis 2:9



## Chapter 6

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/6:1)


ויחשוב שהוא מבני עולם העליון ולא יהיו חשובין בעיניו כל ב"א הדרים בעוהז"ה כי כל העולם כולו הוא כגרגיר חרדל נגד העולם העליון. ויהי' שוה בעיניו אם יאהבוהו או ישנאוהו כי אהבתם ושנאתם אין כלום.


Consider yourself to be a member of the Supernal World and all the inhabitants of this world will be insignificant in your eyes. For the totality of the world is like a mustard seed compared to the Supernal World. It thus should be equal in your eyes whether people love or hate you, because their love and hate is of no significance.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/6:2)


וכן לא ישגיח כלל על תאוות גופו המטונף שהיא צרעת משכא דחוויא:


Likewise, pay no heed to the lusts of your body, which is gross and is the “leprous skin of the snake.”¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ See Zohar Tikkunim, Tikkun 21, 48b.



## Chapter 7

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/7:1)


**ואיתא בזוהר דעבד רעותא דמסכנא.** ויחשוב שהוא עני וידבר תמיד בדברים רכים ותחנונים כמו עני.


It states in Zohar,¹⁶ “One should always strive to be like a pauper.” Thus, always think of yourself as being destitute and speak soft words and supplications, like a pauper.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Zohar Vol. 3, 195a.  It is explained there that the prayers of a pauper always ascend and are favorably received.



## Chapter 8

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/8:1)


ויתבודד תמיד מחשבתו עם השכינה שלא יחשוב רק באהבתו אותה תמיד שהוא מתדבק בו. ויאמר תמיד במחשבתו מתי אזכה שישכון עמי אור השכינה.


Seclude your thoughts constantly with the *Shechinah,* the Divine Presence of *HaShem*. That is, think of nothing but your undying love for her and your desire that she will adhere to you. Always think to yourself, “When will I merit that the light of the Divine Presence of *HaShem* will dwell with me?”



## Chapter 9

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/9:1)


ואם יבאו לו תאוות עוה"ז ירחיק מחשבתו ויבזה התאוה עד שיהי' שנאוי ומאוס אצלו וירגיז יצ"ט על היצה"ר ותאוותו. ובזה יכניעם.


If an extraneous thought of the lusts of this world comes upon you, distance it from your thoughts. Scorn the lust until it becomes hateful and despicable to you. Moreover, incite your good inclination against your evil inclination and its lusts. Through this you will subdue them.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ Talmud Bavli, Brachot 5a.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/9:2)


ושלא יהי' לו עצבות כלל כשלא יהי' לו תאוות עוה"ז. אלא אדרבא ישמח מאד. במה שהוא זוכה להכניע תאוותו לשם כבוד הבורא יתברך כמ"ש חז"ל ושמחים ביסורים.


Now, if you no longer have any of the lusts of this world, do not at all be sad. On the contrary, you should rejoice greatly in having merited to subdue your lusts for the sake of the Name and glory of *HaShem,* the Creator, blessed is He, as our sages, of blessed memory, stated,¹⁸ “And they are joyful in suffering.”

footnotes:
¹⁸ Talmud Bavli, Shabbat 88b.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/9:3)


בזה כאשר לא ימשך אחר תאותו אפילו במחשבה ויבזה אותה ויכניע מאד הקליפות כמ"ש בזוהר ובר לבב דלא אמשיך לרעותיה ולבי' בתר ב"א.


Thus, when you are not drawn after your lusts, even in thought, but instead scorn such thoughts, you greatly lower and subdue the external husks of evil (*Kelipot*). This is as stated in Zohar,¹⁹ “‘A pure heart’²⁰ refers to someone who does not draw his desire and heart to the side of evil (*Sitra Achara*).”

footnotes:
¹⁹ Zohar Vol. 1, 100b.
²⁰ Psalms 24:4



## Chapter 10

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/10:1)


כלל גדול השתוות. ופיר' שיהי' שוה אצלו אם יחזיקוהו לחסרון ידיעה או יודע בכל התורה כולה ודבר הגורם לזה הוא הדביקות בהבורא תדיר. שמחמת טרדות הדביקות אין לו פנאי לחשוב בדברים אלו שטרוד תמיד לדבק עצמו למעלה בו ית'.


Equanimity-*Hishtavut*-*השתוות* is a very important principle from the Baal Shem Tov, may his memory live on for the world to come. That is, it should be equal-*Shaveh*-*שוה* to you whether people consider you to be an ignoramus or knowledgeable in all of Torah. What brings this perspective about is constant adhesion (*Dveikut*) to *HaShem,* the Creator, blessed is He. Because your mind is so preoccupied with adhesion to *HaShem*, you have neither the time nor the interest to think about such matters. For you will be occupied in adhering to *HaShem* above, blessed is He.



## Chapter 11

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/11:1)


ובכל דבר שיעשה יחשוב שהוא עושה בזה נחת רוח לבוראו ית' ולא לצורך עצמו אפילו מעט. אפילו אם עושה שיהי' לו תענוג בעבודתו לא יהי' לצורך עצמו.


In everything you do, think to yourself that through this you are giving satisfaction to *HaShem*, your Creator, blessed is He. That is, do it for *HaShem,* not for yourself, even a little bit. Even if you do it for the delight of serving *HaShem*, this too is for your own self-interest.



## Chapter 12

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/12:1)


ואל יאמר בלבו שהוא גדול מחבירו שהוא עובד בדביקות שהוא כשאר הנבראים שנבראו לצורך עבודתו ית'. והשי"ת נתן לחבירו שכל כמו שנתן לו שכל ובמה הוא חשוב יותר מהתולעת שהתולעת עובד להבורא יתברך בכל שכלו וכחו והאדם ג"כ רמה ותולעה. כמ"ש ואנכי תולעת ולא איש. ואם לא נתן לו השי"ת שכל לא הי' יכול לעובד רק כמו תולעת. וא"כ אפילו מתולעת אינו חשוב במעלה כ"ש מב"א.


Never say in your heart that you are greater than your fellow, that you serve *HaShem* with greater adhesion (*Dveikut*) to Him, because you are like all other creatures that were created to serve *HaShem*, blessed is He. *HaShem*, blessed is He, gave your fellow intellect, just as He gave you intellect (each one according to his measure). How then can you consider yourself to be greater than even a worm? For, unlike you, a worm serves the Creator with all its intellect and strengths. Man, too, is likened to worms and maggots, as the Psalmist states,²¹ “But I am a worm and not a man.” In other words, “Had *HaShem*, blessed is He, not granted me intellect, I too could only serve Him as a worm serves Him. This being so, I am no greater than a worm, let alone other people!”

footnotes:
²¹ Psalms 22:7


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/12:2)


ויחשוב שהוא תולעת ושארי בריות קטנות הם חשובים כמו חבירים בעולם שכולם נבראים ואין להם יכולת רק מה שנתן להם הבורא יתברך ודבר זה יהי' תמיד במחשבתו:


On the contrary, consider yourself, the worm and other small creatures, as equals in the world. We all are created beings and have no abilities except those given to us by *HaShem*, our Creator, blessed is He. This matter should always be in your thoughts.



## Chapter 13

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/13:1)


**אם מתאוה לאיזה עבירה** אז יאמר הפסוקים של העבירה ההוא בטעמים ובנקודות בדחילו ורחימא ואז תלך ממנו התאוה.


If you are roused with lust for a particular sin, God forbid, recite the Torah verses relating to that sin with their intonation and vowels, with fear and love of *HaShem*, blessed is He, and the lust will depart from you.



## Chapter 14

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/14:1)


ואם לאיזה מדה רעה מתאוה ר"ל אז יאמר ששה פעמים ז' עממין. הכנעני והחתי וכו' בכל כחו בדחילו ורחימא ומסתלק ממנו ויקשור המדה הזאת להקב"ה כגון אם יש לו ח"ו אהבה רעה לא יאהב רק את השי"ת וכל מגמתו יהא לזה ואם בכעס שהוא יראה רעה נמשך ממדת הגבורה יגבור על יצרו ויעשה מהמדה הזאת מרכבה להשי"ת.


If lust is roused in you for any evil character traits, God forbid, recite the verse that lists the six nations,²² “The Canaanite, the Hitite etc.” Do so with fear and love of *HaShem*, and it will withdraw from you. Bind the trait to the Holy One, blessed is He. For example, if you are roused with wrong love, God forbid, instead love *HaShem* alone, blessed is He, and invest all your effort into this. If you are roused with anger, which is wicked fear, drawn from the attribute of judgment (*Gevurah*), overpower your inclination and transform it into a vehicle for the Holy One, blessed is He.

footnotes:
²² Exodus 33:2


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/14:2)


וכששומע אחד דורש בדחילו ורחימא אז ידביק לדיבורו מאד ויהא אחדות עמו עם המוכיח כי דיבורו נעשה אצלו מחשבה ובזה תסלק ממנו.


If you hear someone preaching with love and fear of *HaShem*, cleave to his words and become one with the reprover. For, his words will enter your mind and become thought and the spirit of folly will withdraw from you.



## Chapter 15

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/15:1)


**קודם כל יזהר** בכל תנועה ותנועה בעבודתו שיהא בלתי פני' ח"ו ולזה צריך התחכמות גדול עמוק עמוק מי ימצאנה א"כ אין ברירה רק להיות דבר זה לזכרון תמיד ואל יסיח דעתו ממנו אפילו רגע אחד והוא דבר הפוסל בהיסח הדעת.


First and foremost, be careful that every gesture in your service of *HaShem* should be free of ulterior motive, God forbid. This requires great wisdom for,²³ “It is exceedingly deep; who can fathom it?” This being so, there is no choice but to keep it in mind always. Do not remove your attention from it for even an instant, for it is something that becomes invalidated by the removal of attention (*Hese’ach HaDa’at*).²⁴ by Rabbi DovBer of Lubavitch, translated as Essential Faith.

footnotes:
²³ Ecclesiastes 7:24
²⁴ See the introduction to Shaar HaEmunah


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/15:2)


זאת ועוד שנית ליזהר בטבילה בכל עת ולכוון במקוה הכוונה השייכה למקוה.


Secondly, but of equal importance, be careful to immerse in the *Mikvah*(ritual pool) regularly, and to have the intentions that relate to ritual immersion in mind.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/15:3)


והחוט המשולש לא במהרה ינתק וירחיק א"ע מעצבות ויהי' לבו שמח ביה.


The “Three-ply cord not easily severed”²⁵ is to distance yourself from depression. Instead, your heart should rejoice in *HaShem*, blessed is He.

footnotes:
²⁵ Ecclesiastes 4:12



## Chapter 16

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:1)


וכגון דא צריך להודיע להרגיל בחצות לילה.


Likewise, be informed of the importance of accustoming yourself to rise for the midnight prayers.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:2)


ולכל הפחות יזהר להיות התפלה בין בקיץ בין בחורף קודם הנץ החמה דהיינו יהי' רוב התפלה עד קרוב לקריאת שמע קודם הנץ והחילוק בין קודם הנץ בין לאחר הנץ כרחוק מזרח ממערב. כי אז עדיין יכולין לבטל.


At the very least, take care to begin your prayers before sunrise, whether in summer or winter. That is, most of the prayers leading up to the recital of *Shema* should be before sunrise. The difference between prayers recited before sunrise and those recited after sunrise are like the distance between east and west, because before sunrise one can still nullify all judgments. For once the sun rises, all proclamations concerning that day have already been issued, whether for good or for bad, whereas before the proclamation, they can still be rescinded and nullified.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:3)


וסימנך והוא כחתן יוצא מחופתו ישיש כגבור וכו' ואין נסתר מֵחַמָתו אל תקרי מֵחַמָתו אלא מֵחֵימָתו. כלומר כשכבר השמש יצא על הארץ אזי אין נסתר מהדינים הבאים ממלאכי חימה. ע"כ אל יהא בעיניך דבר זה קטן כי גדול הוא.


The mnemonic by which to remember this, is the verse,²⁶ “He has set up a tent for the sun, which is like a groom emerging from his bridal chamber, it rejoices like a powerful warrior to run its course… nothing is hidden from its heat-*Chamato*-*מחמתו*.” Do not read, “From its heat-*MeChamato*-*מחמתו*,” but “from its wrath-*MeCheimato*-*מחימתו*.” In other words, once the sun has come out over the earth, none can hide from the judgments rendered by the angels of wrath. Therefore, do not take this matter lightly, for it is of great importance.

footnotes:
²⁶ Psalms 19:5-7


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/16:4)


ובעש"ט ז"ל הי' מדקדק גדול ע"ז ולפעמים כשהי' עת שלא הי' לו מנין הי' מתפלל ביחיד:


The Baal Shem Tov, of blessed memory, was extremely careful in this, to such an extent that, at times, when a minyan of ten Jews were not present, he would instead pray privately.



## Chapter 17

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:1)


**לא להניח שום יום** בלא מצוה הן קלה הן חמורה כמו שאמרו רז"ל הוי זהיר במצוה קלה כבחמורה. זהיר מלשון והמשכילים יזהירו וכו' דהיינו שהנשמ' תאיר ותזהיר ממצוה קלה כבחמורה כי רחמנ' ליבא בעי.


Do not let a day to pass without fulfilling a *Mitzvah*-commandment, whether it is a minor *mitzvah* or a major one, like our sages, of blessed memory, stated,²⁷ “Be as radiant-*Zahir*-*זהיר* with a minor *mitzvah* as with a major one.” Though the word *Zahir*-*זהיר*is usually translated as “careful,” it is of the same root, as in the verse,²⁸ “And they that are wise will be radiant-*Yazhiru*-*יזהירו*like the Zohar*-זהר*-radiance of the firmament.” In other words, the soul should be as radiant and illuminated from the performance of a minor *mitzvah* as a major one, for “the Merciful One seeks the heart.”²⁹

footnotes:
²⁷ Mishnah Avot 2:1
²⁸ Daniel 12:3
²⁹ Zohar Vol. 2, 162b; Talmud Bavli, Sanhedrin 106b.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:2)


והוא דבר גדול מאד כי אז ידע שעשה פעולה ביום זה שברא מלאך אחד ואם יש עליו מלאך מליץ אחד וכו'.


This is a very great matter, for through it you will know that you have affected the day and created an angel. As the verse states,³⁰ “Even if there is only a single advocating angel, out of a thousand, to declare man’s uprightness on his behalf; *HaShem* will be gracious to him and say, ‘Redeem him from going down into the grave. I have found atonement for him.’”

footnotes:
³⁰ Job 33:23-24


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:3)


וסימנך שומר מצוה לא ידע דבר רע. היינו. כשאדם מקבל על עצמו דבר זה אזי צריך להיות עומד על משמרתו מהבוקר עד הערב אולי יזדמן לו מצוה. וזהו שומר מלשון ואביו שמר את הדבר אזי הסגולה שלא ידע דבר רע ח"ו. היינו שלא יבא לידי קרי הנקרא רע ח"ו.


The mnemonic by which to remember this, is the verse,³¹ “He who guards-*Shomer*-*שומר* the *mitzvah* will know no evil.” In other words, whoever accepts this matter upon himself, must stand guard at his post from morning to evening, awaiting the opportunity to fulfill any *mitzvah* that comes his way. That is, the term guard-*Shomer*-*שומר* is of the same root used in the verse,³² “His father waited in anticipation-*Shamar*-*שמר* of the matter.”³³ Through this, one merits that, “He will know no evil.” That is, he will not be subject to random happenstance (*Keri-קרי*)³⁴ which is called evil, God forbid.

footnotes:
³¹ Ecclesiastes 8:5
³² Genesis 37:11
³³ Rashi to Genesis 37:11
³⁴ Leviticus 26:21 & Rashi there; Also see Ginat Egoz of Rabbi Yosef Gikatilla, translated as HaShem Is One, Volume 2, pg. 353.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 17:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/17:4)


וסימנך חסד אל כל היום היינו שבכל יום ויום צריך לעשות חסד לאל ית':


The mnemonic by which to remember this is the verse,³⁵ “The kindness of God is all day long.” That is, one must do acts of lovingkindness for God, blessed is He, every single day.

footnotes:
³⁵ Psalms 52:3



## Chapter 18

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/18:1)


**לשמור שבת כהלכתו** בכל דקדוקי' ופרטי' וסימנך תשב אנוש עד דכא. תשב אותיות שבת אנוש היינו אפילו עובד ע"ז כדור אנוש.


Keep the Shabbat strictly, according to the *Halachah*,³⁶ with all its details and specifics. The mnemonic by which to remember this, is the verse,³⁷ “You turn back-*Tashev*-*תשב*man-*Enosh*-*אנוש* into pulp.” The word “You turn back-*Tashev*-*תשב*” consists of the same letters as, “Shabbat-*שבת*.” The word used for man here is, “*Enosh*-*אנוש*” indicating that, “Even if he is an idolater, like the generation of *Enosh,*he will be forgiven.”³⁸ who does this, and the person who holds strong to it, one who guards the Shabbat from desecrating it-*Mechalelo*, and guards his hand from doing any evil.’ (Isaiah 56:2). Do not read ‘from desecrating it-*Mechalelo-*מחללו,’ but rather, ‘he is forgiven-*Machul Lo-*מחל לו.’”

footnotes:
³⁶ Torah law
³⁷ Psalms 90:3
³⁸ Talmud Bavli, Shabbat 118b; See Genesis 4:26; Mishneh Torah, Hilchot Avoda Zara Ch. 1 – Rabbi Chiyah bar Abba said that Rabbi Yochanan said: Anyone who observes Shabbat in accordance with its laws, even if he worships idolatry as in the generation of Enosh, he is forgiven, as it states, ‘Fortunate is the man-Enosh



## Chapter 19

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/19:1)


ולכל הפחות לדקדק בק"ש הנאמר פעמים בכל יום ויום לאומרה בלי מחשבה זרה ח"ו והוא דבר גדול לאין ערך וסימנך כל הקורא ק"ש מזיקין בדילין וכו' מזיקין היינו המזיקי' שבעולם הן בגופם הן בנפשם.


At the very least, be scrupulous with the *Shema* that is recited twice daily, taking care to recite it without any extraneous thoughts, God forbid. This is an important matter of immeasurable greatness. The mnemonic by which to remember this is the statement,³⁹ “Whoever recites the *Shema* upon his bed, the forces of harm (*Mazikin*⁴⁰מזיקין generally refers to the demonic forces, but is used here to refer to anything that may cause harm in the world.) depart from him.” The term, “the forces of harm-*Mazikin*-*מזיקין*” means all forms of harm in the world, whether bodily harm or spiritual harm.

footnotes:
³⁹ Talmud Bavli, Brachot 5a
⁴⁰ Talmudic usage of the term Mazikin-


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/19:2)


הגם שאי אפשר להתפלל בלי מחשבה זרה ירגיל עצמו בלי מחשבה זרה בתחלה:


Even if you find it impossible to pray entirely without extraneous thoughts, train yourself to, at least, begin without extraneous thoughts.⁴¹

footnotes:
⁴¹ And you will become progressively better at it.



## Chapter 20

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/20:1)


**יאחוז במדות זריזות מאד** יקום בזריזות משינתו כי נתחדש ונעשה אדם אחר והוא ראוי להוליד. ונעשה כמדת הקב"ה שהוליד עולמות. וכן כל דבר שעושה יעשה בזריזות. כי בכל דבר יכול לעבוד את ה'.


Embrace the trait of zeal very much. Rise with zeal and alacrity from your sleep with the realization that you have been created anew and have become a new man, capable of producing offspring.⁴² Thus, you are likened to the attribute of the Holy One, blessed is He, who has given birth to worlds. Thus, everything you do should be with zeal and enthusiasm, because *HaShem* can be served in everything.

footnotes:
⁴² One’s deeds are compared to the birthing of offspring.  See Rashi to Genesis 6:9. Also see Sefer HaMashalim of Rabbi Yosef Gikatilla, translated as The Book of Allegories, Num. 38.



## Chapter 21

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/21:1)


וכשמניח הטלית יראה את התכלת פירוש שתפול עליו יראה.


When you don the prayer shawl (*Talit*), “Observe the *Techeilet-*blue string.”⁴³ The explanation of this is that fear of *HaShem* should come upon you.⁴⁴-commandment merits receiving the Divine Presence. For with respect to the fringes-*Tzitzit*it is written, ‘That you may look upon it-*u’Re’item Oto*-וראיתם אותו’ (Num. 15:39) and it is written, ‘You shall fear *HaShem*, your God, and Him you shall serve-*Et HaShem Elohecha Tirah, V’Oto Ta’avod*-את ה׳ אלהי״ך תירא ואותו תעבוד.’ (Deut. 6:13).”

footnotes:
⁴³ Numbers 15:38
⁴⁴ Talmud Bavli, Menachot 43b: “Rabbi Shimon Bar Yochai says: Anyone who is diligent in this Mitzvah



## Chapter 22

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/22:1)


וכל מה שרואה יזכור בהקב"ה אם אהבה יזכור באהבת ה' ואם יראה יזכור ביראת ה' כידוע בכמה מקומות. ואפילו כשהולך לבה"כ יחשוב הלא אני מפריד רע מטוב. וזהו יחודים והטוב ישאר לעבודתו יתברך.


Whatever you experience, remember the Holy One, blessed is He. If you experience love, recall the love of *HaShem*, if you experience fear, recall the fear of *HaShem*, as known from many sources. Even before going to the restroom, think to yourself, “Am I not separating bad from good and retaining the good for the service of *HaShem*, blessed is He?” This is the matter of unifying everything to *HaShem* (*Yichudim*). Similarly, when you go to sleep, think to yourself, “By doing this my intellect will ascend to the Holy One, blessed is He, and will be rejuvenated and strengthened for His service.”⁴⁵

footnotes:
⁴⁵ See Mishneh Torah, Hilchot De’ot 3:3; Also see Ginat Egoz of Rabbi Yosef Gikatilla, Shaar HaYichud-The Gate of Unification.



## Chapter 23

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/23:1)


וכשהולך לישן יחשוב הלא המוחין שלו ילכו להקב"ה ויתחזקו לעבודתו ית'. קודם השינה ישכב באימה וביראה וירגיז וירעד מאימת הבורא ית' ולא יבא לידי חטא כמ"ש רגזו ואל תחטאו:


Before falling asleep, lie down with dread and awe, quaking and trembling from the fear of the Creator, blessed is He. Through this you will not come to sin, as the verse states,⁴⁶ “Tremble and sin not, reflect in your hearts while on your beds.”

footnotes:
⁴⁶ Psalms 4:5; Talmud Bavli, Brachot 5a; Zohar Vol. 3, 113b



## Chapter 24

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/24:1)


**מחשבתו תהי' למעלה בעולם העליון** בעבודת השי"ת וידבק ויבטח בו שהוא ישיג חפצו.


Direct your thoughts to the Supernal World above in service of *HaShem*, blessed is He. Adhere to *HaShem* and trust that He will achieve His desire.⁴⁷

footnotes:
⁴⁷ See Ginat Egoz of Rabbi Yosef Gikatilla, Shaar HaYichud-The Gate of Unification.  Also see HaShem is One, Volume 2, wherein it is extensively explained that the Upper World refers to the World of Intellect.



## Chapter 25

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/25:1)


זהו כלל גדול באות' מצוה שהתחיל בה כשקם ממטתו ילך בה כל היום ולא במחשבה אחרת:


This is a great principle: The thought one begins with when arising from bed, will accompany him throughout the day, rather than another thought.⁴⁸

footnotes:
⁴⁸ See Poke’ach Ivrim of Rabbi DovBer of Lubavitch, translated as Opening the Eyes of the Blind, Sections 1 & 2.



## Chapter 26

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/26:1)


**יהא זהיר מאד לקום תמיד בחצות הלילה** אם אינו קם והי' שלא מחמ' אונס הוא בנידוי למעלה ח"ו.


Be very scrupulous about always rising for the midnight prayers. If you did not rise, and you were not prevented because of something beyond your control, you will be ostracized Above, God forbid.⁴⁹

footnotes:
⁴⁹ The importance of Tikkun Chatzot and nightly Torah study is mentioned in the Talmud and throughout the Zohar.  In Iggeret HaTeshuvah of Tanya, Ch. 10, it is prescribed that “Whoever cannot do this nightly should at the very least do so once every week, before the Shabbat.”  Also see Shulchan Aruch (the Code of Jewish Law), Orach Chayim 1:2-4.



## Chapter 27

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/27:1)


ויהפוך הלילות לימים ויישן ביום כמה שעות כדי שתספיק לו מעט שינה בלילה:


Convert your nights into days. Sleep a few hours during the day, so that a small amount of sleep at night will suffice you.



## Chapter 28

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/28:1)


**כשקם בחצו' לילה** ונופל עליו שינה ילך לכאן ולכאן בבית וישיר בשירות ויגביה קולו כדי להסיר מעליו השינה.


If you rise at midnight and are overcome with drowsiness, you should pace back and forth in the house, sing songs of praise to *HaShem* and raise your voice, thus removing drowsiness from yourself.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/28:2)


ילמוד הרבה שיעורים ולא הכל בענין אחד שיהא נראה עליו כמשאוי רק ילמוד הרבה ענינים.


Moreover, study several Torah subjects, rather than concentrating on one subject, and it will not become burdensome to you. Better to study several Torah subjects, for this will assist in removing drowsiness.



## Chapter 29

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/29:1)


וכשלומד צריך לנוח מעט בכל שעה כדי לדבק עצמו בו ית'.


When studying Torah, pause and rest a bit every hour or so, in order to bond yourself to *HaShem*, blessed is He. Nevertheless, one must study Torah.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/29:2)


אעפ"י שבשעת הלימוד א"א לדבק עצמו בו ית' מ"מ צריך ללמוד. והתורה מצחצחה נשמתו והוא עץ חיים למחזיקים בה. ואם לא ילמוד יבוטל דביקותו. ויחשוב שכמו בעת השינה אינו יכול להיות דבוק וכן כשנופלים המוחים שלו כן לא יהי' בעת הלימוד גרוע מהם. רק מ"מ צריך ליישב עצמו בכל שעה ורגע דביקות הבורא ית' כנ"ל:


Even though when you are immersed in the study of Torah it is not possible for you to bond yourself to *HaShem*, blessed is He, nonetheless, you must study Torah. This is because the Torah purifies the soul and is,⁵⁰ “A tree of life for those who grasp it.” Thus, if you do not study Torah, your adhesion-*Dveikut* to *HaShem* will dissipate and become null and void. Therefore, when studying Torah, think to yourself that just as you cannot be in a state of adhesion (*Dveikut*) during sleep or when your intellectual faculties fall to a state of lesser awareness, so also, when you study Torah, it is no worse than this. Nonetheless, you should stop and center yourself to bond to *HaShem*, the Creator, blessed is He, every once in a while, as said above.

footnotes:
⁵⁰ Proverbs 3:18



## Chapter 30

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/30:1)


**בעת הלימוד** כשמדבר צריך שלא לחשוב מחשבה אחרת רק דביקות הבורא ית' ובעת הלימוד צריך לחשוב מחשבות הלימוד ומכח זה יבא לו דבוק באלקות כראוי.


When speaking in conversation, think only of adhesion-*Dveikut* to *HaShem*, the Creator, blessed is He, rather than other thoughts. When studying Torah, you must think about the subject you are learning, and through this you will properly cleave to Godliness.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/30:2)


וצריך בכל פעם לעסוק בתורה שהוא עץ חיים למחזיקים בה. אבל כשמדבר רק דברים בטלים ויסמוך על הדביקות יזהר מאוד שלא יפול לפעמים מהדביקות.


One must always be occupied with the study of Torah, for it is,⁵¹ “A tree of life for those who grasp it.” However, if you are engaged in mundane conversation and are relying on your adhesion-*Dveikut* to *HaShem,* be very careful not to fall at times, from your adhesion to Him.

footnotes:
⁵¹ Proverbs 3:18



## Chapter 31

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/31:1)


[ריבעש"ט אמר כשהוא דבוק בבורא ית' ונופל לו מחשבה מאיזה דבר מסתמא הוא כך כמו שנופל לו במחשבתו וזהו רוה"ק מעט. גם אמר]


Rabbi Yisroel Baal Shem Tov said that when he is in a state of adhesion (*Dveikut*) to *HaShem,* the Creator, blessed is He, and a thought about something falls into his mind, it presumably is just as it arose in his thought. This is a measure of Divine Inspiration (*Ru’ach HaKodesh*).


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/31:2)


מכח התורה שלמד באותו יום. ואח"כ נזדמן לו דבר לעשות ואינו יודע אם לעשות אם לא. יכול להבין מכח אותו הענין שלמד איך יעשה ובלבד שיהי' תמיד דבוק בהשי"ת אז יזמין לו השי"ת תמיד שידע מכח התורה.


He also said that from the power of the Torah that a person learns on a given day, when he has the opportunity to do something, but does not know if he should do it or not, he can understand how to proceed through the power of the Torah subject that he learned. This holds true on condition that he is in a constant state of adhesion-*Dveikut* to *HaShem*, blessed is He. If this is so, *HaShem* will arrange that he will know what to do through the power of the Torah.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 31:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/31:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/31:3)


אבל אדם שהלך בקרי עם השי"ת אז השי"ת הולך עמו ג"כ באקראי וגם אינו מזמין לו מלבושים ומאכלים שיש בהם ניצוצות השייכים לשורש נשמתו לתקנם:


In contrast, if a person relates to *HaShem* by way of happenstance, then *HaShem* likewise relates to him through happenstance.⁵² Moreover*, HaShem* does not arrange garments and sustenance for him that contain those sparks of Godliness that relate to the root of his soul, so that he may rectify them.

footnotes:
⁵² Leviticus 26:21 & Rashi there; Also see Ginat Egoz of Rabbi Yosef Gikatilla, translated as HaShem Is One, Volume 2, pg. 353.



## Chapter 32

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/32:1)


**צריך לילך ממדריגה למדריגה בתפלה** שלא יפסיד כל כחו בתחלת התפלה רק יתחיל במתון ובאמצע התפלה ידבק בדביקות גדול אז יהי' יכול לומר אפילו במרוצה התיבות של התפלה.


It is important to gradually ascend from level to level in prayer,⁵³ so that all your strength is not exhausted at the beginning of your prayers. Rather, begin with moderation, and then, in the middle of your prayers, bond yourself with great adhesion to *HaShem*through the words of prayer. You will then even be able to say the words of prayer quickly, without breaking your bond.

footnotes:
⁵³ See Kuntres Inyan Tefilah by Rabbi DovBer of Lubavitch, translated as Praying with Passion.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/32:2)


אע"פ שבתחילת התפלה אינו יכול להתפלל בדביקות מ"מ יאמר התיבות בכוונה גדולה ויחזק א"ע מעט מעט עד שיעזור לו השי"ת להתפלל בדביקות גדול:


Even though at the beginning of prayer you cannot pray with such great adhesion to *HaShem*, nevertheless, say the words with great concentration of intention and strengthen yourself little by little, until *HaShem*, blessed is He, will assist you to pray with great adhesion to Him.



## Chapter 33

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/33:1)


צריך האדם ללמוד ולהרגיל א"ע שיתפלל אפילו הזמירות בקול נמוך ויצעק בלחש ויאמר הדבר בין בזמירות בין בלימוד בכל כחו כדכתיב כל עצמותי תאמרנה. והצעקה שתהי' מחמת דביקות תהי' בלחש:


A person must train and accustom himself to pray in a low voice and to cry out silently, even when reciting the songs of praise. Nonetheless, he should say the words, whether of prayer or of Torah, with all his might, as the verse states,⁵⁴ “All my bones⁵⁵-כל עצמותי-All my bones also means “My very essence.” shall say, ‘*HaShem*, who is like You.’” A cry that results from true adhesion to *HaShem* is a silent cry.⁵⁶) and divine inspiration during prayer, see Kuntres HaHitpaalut of Rabbi DovBer of Lubavitch, translated as Divine Inspiration.

footnotes:
⁵⁴ Psalms 35:10
⁵⁵ Kol Atzmotai
⁵⁶ For an extensive explanation of the varying levels of adhesion (Dveikut



## Chapter 34

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/34:1)


**דע כי כל תיבה הוא קומה שלימה** וצריך להיות כל כחו בה כי אם לאו תהי' כמו מחוסר אבר.


Know that every word of prayer is a complete stature (*Komah Shleimah*). Therefore, invest all your strength into it, for otherwise, it is missing a limb.



## Chapter 35

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/35:1)


זהו חסד גדול מהשי"ת שאדם חי אחר התפלה. שלפי דרך הטבע הי' לו למות מחמת שמפסיד כחו בתפלה כי נתן כחו בהתפלה כל כך מכח הכוונות הגדולות שהוא מכווין:


It is a great kindness from *HaShem*, blessed is He, that a person lives after prayer. For, with the strength of his great concentration and intention, he exhausts all his strength to such a degree, that according to the natural order, he should have died.



## Chapter 36

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/36:1)


**פעמים יכול לומר התפלה במהירות גדול מאד** מחמת שבוער בלבו אהבת השם מאד והתיבות יוצאים מפיו מעצמם.


At times a person is able to pray very quickly because his heart is burning with such intense love of *HaShem*, blessed is He, that the words come out of his mouth of their own accord.



## Chapter 37

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/37:1)


בתפלה בלחש כשהוא מדבק עצמו למעלה אז זוכה ומעלין אותו יותר למעלה באותה תפלה כמ"ש רז"ל הבא לטהר מסייעין אותו וע"י התפלה זו זוכה שידבק במחשבתו למעלה. ומכח זה יוכל לבא עוד למדריגה יותר גדולה אפילו שלא בשעת התפלה שיהא דבוק למעלה:


If a person bonds himself to *HaShem*above during the silent prayer,⁵⁷ silent prayer also called the *Amidah*-standing prayer. he could then merit to be elevated even higher during that prayer, as our sages, of blessed memory, taught,⁵⁸ “One who comes to purify himself is granted assistance.” Thus, through the strength of this prayer, in which he merits to attain adhesion of thought to *HaShem* above, he can merit to attain an even greater level, which is to be in a state of adhesion-*Dveikut*to *HaShem*above, even while not engaged in prayer.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ The Shemonah Esreh
⁵⁸ Talmud Bavli, Shabbat 104a



## Chapter 38

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/38:1)


**לא יאמר הרבה תהלים קודם התפלה** שלא יחליש גופו ולא יוכל אח"כ לומר העיקר ר"ל חובת היום דהיינו זמירות וק"ש ותפלה בדביקות גדול מחמת שהפסיד כחו קודם התפלה בשאר דברים. רק תחלה יאמר העיקר בדביקות אם יתן לו השי"ת עוד כח יאמר תהלים ושיר השירים בדביקות.


Before praying, do not recite many Psalms with great adhesion to *HaShem*, because by doing so you weaken your body and will thus be unable to recite the essential, obligatory prayers of the day with great adhesion, namely, the verses of song, the *Shema* and the *Amidah*prayer. By doing so, you would be exhausting your strength with other things before beginning the prayers. Instead, first recite the primary prayers with adhesion-*Dveikut* to *HaShem* and then, if *HaShem*, blessed is He, grants you additional strength, recite Psalms and the Song of Songs with adhesion to Him afterwards.



## Chapter 39

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/39:1)


קודם נעילה ביוה"כ יאמר מחזור בקטנות כדי שיוכל אח"כ להתפלל בדביקות:


Before *Ne’ilah* (The concluding prayer of *Yom Kippur-* the Day of Atonement) reserve your strength and recite the *Machzor*⁵⁹liturgy in a diminished state of Godly awareness-*Katnut*.⁶⁰ In this way, when you come to *Ne’ilah*, you will have the strength to recite it with great adhesion-*Dveikut* to *HaShem.*

footnotes:
⁵⁹ The Yom Kippur
⁶⁰ See chapters 34 & 35 of Shaar HaYichud of Rabbi DovBer of Lubavitch, translated with commentary as The Gate of Unity, as well as the different levels described in Kuntres HaHitpaalut translated as Divine Inspiration.



## Chapter 40

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/40:1)


**כשאדם במדריגה קטנה** אז טוב לו יותר להתפלל מתוך הסידור שמכח שרואה האותיות מתפלל יותר בכוונה.


If you are in a diminished state of mind, it is better to pray from the prayer book, rather than from memory, for by seeing the words and letters of the prayers, you will pray with greater intention and devotion.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 40:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/40:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/40:2)


אבל כשהוא דבוק בעולם העליון אז יותר טוב לו לסגור עיניו כדי שלא יבטל אותו הראי' מלהיות דבוק בעולם העליון.


However, if you are in a state of adhesion to the Upper World, it is better to close your eyes, so that the sense of vision will not obstruct your adhesion to the Upper World.



## Chapter 41

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 41:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/41:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/41:1)


הנשמה אמרה להרב שמה שזכה שנתגלו אליו דברים העליונים לא מפני שלמד הרבה מש"ס ופוסקים. רק משום תפלה שהי' מתפלל תמיד בכוונה גדולה ומשם זכה למעלה עליונה:


The soul⁶¹ told the Rabbi (Rabbi Yisroel Baal Shem Tov) that the reason he merited all the Supernal revelations that were revealed to him was not because of the great amount of Talmud and codifiers that he studied, but rather, because of his prayers. For, he always prayed with tremendous devotion, and this is why he merited such a lofty level.

footnotes:
⁶¹ This refers to the upper aspect of his own soul.  Such revelations of the upper aspect of one’s soul are elucidated in Shaarei Kedushah of Rabbi Chaim Vital, Section 3, Ch. 5, translated as Gates of Holiness.



## Chapter 42

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/42:1)


**יחשוב קודם התפלה** שהוא מוכן למות באותה התפלה מחמת הכוונה. ויש מכוונים כ"כ עד שפעמים בטבע הי' יכול למות בב' או בג' תיבות שאומר לפניו יתברך.


Before praying, think to yourself that you are prepared to die during prayer due to the intense adhesion of your mind to *HaShem*.⁶² There are those who pray with such great intention that, at times, according to the natural order, they could have died just by saying two or three words in *HaShem’s*presence, blessed is He.

footnotes:
⁶² See Shaarei Orah of Rabbi Yosef Gikatilla, Chapter 1.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 42:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/42:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/42:2)


וכשיחשוב כך יאמר בלבו למה לי איזה פני' וגאוה באותה תפלה כיון שבדעתו אפי' למות אחר ב' או ג' תיבות. ובאמת הוא חסד גדול שהשי"ת נותן לו כח ומשלים התפלה והוא חי.


However, when you think this, say in your heart, “Since I am ready to die even after two or three words, why should I have any self-interest or pride in this prayer?” In reality, it is a very great kindness that *HaShem*, blessed is He, gives us the strength to complete our prayers and remain alive.



## Chapter 43

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:1)


יכוין בתענית אוי לי בשביל תאוותי וכבודי הסרוח הכעסתי את המלך העליון וכו' לכך אני רוצה לענות את עצמי להכניע תאוותי וכבודי ואגרום למעלה שיכנע עבד תחת רבו ושפחה תחת גבירתה ולקיים מצות תשובה.


When undergoing a fast of abstinence, have the following in mind: “Woe is me! Because of my lusts and repugnant pride, I have angered the Supernal King! Therefore, I want to afflict myself and subdue my evil inclination, my lusts and pride, and affect the Supernal realms so that, “The servant will be subdued under his Master and the maid-servant under her Mistress.”⁶³ Through this, I fulfill the commandment-*Mitzvah* of repentance-*Teshuvah*.

footnotes:
⁶³ See Proverbs 30:22-23


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:2)


והריני רוצה לענות עצמי כדי שאוכל לעבוד את השי"ת באמת ובלב שלם באהבה ויראה כדי שיהי' יחודו על ידי לכך אני רוצה לענות א"ע וכדי להקריב א"ע לקרבן לפניו.


I wish to afflict myself so that I will be able to serve *HaShem*, blessed is He, truthfully and wholeheartedly, with love and fear, so that His unity shall be actualized by my hand. This is why I wish to afflict myself and to bring myself as an offering before Him.”


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:3)


אוי לי מה אני ומה חיי שאני רוצה להקריב חלבי ודמי וגופי ואשי ורוחי ונפשי ונשמתי וכחי ולבי וזרועותי (בפומי ולבי ורעותא) לפניו לפני בורא כל העולמו'. וכל העולמות נעשו בדבריו והכל כאין נגדו ומכ"ש אני עפר רמה ותולעה ואין לי לבקש רק מחסדיו הגדולים שיוסיף לי כח להקריב הרבה קרבנות לפניו.


“Woe is me! What am I and what is my life? I wish to offer my fat, my blood, my body, my fire, my spirit, my soul, my strength, my heart, and my desires before *HaShem*, blessed is He, before the Creator of all worlds. All the worlds were brought about by His word, and are as nothing compared to Him. Certainly, I too am no more than dust, worm, and maggot. All I can request of His abundant kindness is that He grant me additional strength to bring many more offerings before Him.”


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:4)


ויפה לי לשמוח עצמי מאוד שזכיתי שנעשו מגופי רוחי ונפשי כמה נחת רוח ועוד יפה לי לשמוח עצמי שנתן לנו דבר שנוכל להכניע את היצה"ר מעלינו. ואני רוצה לענות עצמי בשביל שצערתי לקוב"ה ושכינתיה וכדי להקל צערה ג"כ אני רוצה לענות א"ע.


“I should rejoice greatly to have merited that my body, spirit and soul give satisfaction of spirit to the Creator, blessed is He. Moreover, I should rejoice that He has given the means by which subdue the evil inclination. I wish to afflict myself because I caused sorrow to the Holy One, blessed is He, and His Divine Presence, the *Shechinah*. Therefore, I want to diminish their sorrow.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:5)


אוי לי מה נחשב ענויי נגד מה שצערתי אותו זה כמה שנים ואין לי לבקש רק מחסדיו הגדולים שישגיח על עינויי להקל צער משכינתי' ויסיר הקליפות מעלינו.


Woe is me! What value can my afflictions possibly have compared to the sorrow I have caused over the years. All I can ask is that in His abundant kindnesses *HaShem* should take note of my affliction and diminish the sorrow of His *Shechinah-*The Divine Presence. On account of my afflictions may He remove the external husks of evil (*Kelipot*) from upon us.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:6)


ע"י עינוי שלי ואגרום למעלה שיסורו כל הקליפות מהשכינה ותטהר ותיחד עם בעלה ביחוד גמור בחסד וברחמים ויהי' יחוד מעילא לתתא ע"י עינויי ממוח ללב ומלב לכבד וירק שפעו גם עלי.


May my afflictions cause the removal of all the external husks of evil (*Kelipot*) from the *Shechinah* above, thus purifying and unifying her to her ‘Husband,’ the Holy One, blessed is He, in complete unity, with kindness and mercy. Through my afflictions, let there be a unification from above to below, from the mind to the heart and from the heart to the liver, and may He pour His influence upon me too.”


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:7)


ואני בוטח בו שהוא ברא כל העולמות בדבריו יש מאין והכל כאין נגדו והוא משגיח עליהם לתת להם שפע וחיותם וכ"ש שיכול להוריד לי כח וישגיח בחסדיו גם עלי ויצילני שלא יבטל אותי היצה"ר בשום דבר בענויי שיאמר לי שאני חלש ונתייבשו המוחין שלי וכן כמה פיתויים ולכך יש לי לבקש כח מחסדיו הגדולים ושהוא נתן בלבי לעשות עצתי ועזרני כמה פעמים בחסדיו. גם היום יעזור לי ויצילנו מכל דברים המבטלים אותי.


“I put my trust in Him, for He created all worlds and brought them into being from nothing, and compared to Him, all is as nothing. He supervises over them all, bestowing them with beneficence and life. Therefore, He can certainly draw down additional strength to me and watch over me, to save me in His kindness, so that the evil inclination will not thwart me in any way from my self-affliction, by telling me that I am too weak, that my intellect is affected by the fast and that, because of that, I will be incapable of serving Him as fitting, and other such foolish excuses. Thus, all I can do is request His great kindness, for He is the One who put it into my heart to afflict myself and in His kindness, He has helped me on many occasions. May He help me today, as well, and save me from anything that wishes to obstruct me.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:8)


וגם מצדי אין נחשב עינוי שהכל מאתו ית' אבל מצידי לא הייתי יכול לענות א"ע כלל ואני מוסר עצמי להבורא כל העולמות בדיבורו אפילו לסבול כל העינויים וכל הבזיונות לשם יחודו והריני רוצה לקיים והייתים קדושים. ואני בוטח בחסדיו שיוסיף לי כח שאוכל לעבוד אותו באמת ויעזרני שלא ידעו בני אדם ממעשי.


From my own perspective this cannot even be considered affliction, since everything is from *HaShem*, blessed is He, and on my own I would be incapable of afflicting myself altogether. I thus give myself over to the Singular One who creates all the worlds with His speech, and I am willing to bear every type of affliction and disgrace for the sake of His unity. I desire to fulfill His command,⁶⁴ “You shall sanctify yourselves and be holy,” and I trust that, in His kindness, He will grant me the strength to serve Him in truth. May He assist me so that others should not know of my deeds.”

footnotes:
⁶⁴ Leviticus 20:7


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:9)


ואין אני מתיירא מחולשה מחמת התענית כי בלא זה כמה ב"א חולים. ועוד שהשכינה סועד את החולה שנאמר ד' יסעדנו על ערש דוי בחסדיו יסעדנו ג"כ ואני הולך בדרך כל הארץ ואין אני משנה.


“I do not fear any weakness that may arise on account of my fast, for many people become ill even without fasting. Moreover, the *Shechinah* sustains those who are ill,⁶⁵ as it states,⁶⁶ “*HaShem* sustains him on the sickbed.” Thus, *HaShem*, in His kindness, will sustain me. Moreover, in doing so,⁶⁷ “I am going the way of all the earth,” and am not deviating from the standard.

footnotes:
⁶⁵ Talmud Bavli, Shabbat 12b
⁶⁶ Psalms 41:4
⁶⁷ Kings I 2:2


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:10)


וחכמים אמרו כל הגדול מחבירו יצרו גדול ממנו וכיון שאין אני משנה אני יכול לבטוח בו. בטח בד' ועשה טוב שעל המצות רשאי לבטוח וקווי ה' יחליפו כח. וגם המת מתוך תשובה סימן יפה לו ואפשר שבלא זה אהי' צריך להתגלגל על עונות ושלא עבדתי כראוי באהבה וביראה. וגם אל תדאג צרת מחר.


In addition, our sages stated that,⁶⁸ “Whoever is greater than his fellow, his evil inclination is also greater.” Thus, since I am not deviating from the standard, I can trust *HaShem* in this, as it states,⁶⁹ “Trust in *HaShem* and do good.” For, when it comes to the fulfillment of the commandments, it is permissible for a person to place his trust in *HaShem*, as it states,⁷⁰ “Those who hope in *HaShem* will have renewed strength.” Likewise, one who dies while engaged in repentance-*Teshuvah*, it is a good sign for him.⁷¹ For, were it not for this, it is quite possible that I would need to be reincarnated for my sins, or on account of not having served *HaShem* with love and with fear, as is fitting. Additionally, it states that,⁷² “A person should not worry about future troubles.”

footnotes:
⁶⁸ Talmud Bavli, Sukkah 52a
⁶⁹ Psalms 37:3
⁷⁰ Isaiah 40:31
⁷¹ Talmud Bavli, Ketuvot 103b; Avodah Zarah 17a; Avot d’Rabbi Natan, 25:2
⁷² Talmud Bavli, Yevamot 63b


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:11)


ועיקר התשובה שישוב מדרכיו הרעים.


The essence of repentance-*Teshuvah* is to turn away from your evil ways.⁷³

footnotes:
⁷³ Talmud Bavli, Taanit 16a; Mishneh Torah, Laws of Fasting 5:1; For an extensive discourse on repentance see Derech Chayim by Rabbi DovBer of Lubavitch, translated as The Way of Life.  For a practical guide to repentance see his Poke’ach Ivrim translated as Opening the Eyes of the Blind.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:12)


גם לא יתגאה שאם מתגאה בתעניתא לכלבא אתמסר.


Moreover, do not be prideful, for whoever is prideful in his abstinence, his abstinence, “is given over to the dog.”⁷⁴).

footnotes:
⁷⁴ Zohar, Tikkunim, Tikkun 18, 33b – “One whose offering is blemished and not as it should be, it is given over to the dog, and his requests and supplications are pushed aside,” that is, they are given over to the external forces of evil (Kelipot


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:13)


ואפילו יחיד שעשה תשובה מוחלין לכל העולם. וכיון שכן ישמח ביסורי התענית שמסר נפשו על זה שבוודאי יפעל דבר גדול.


On the other hand, “Even if only a single person does *Teshuvah-*repentance, the entire world is forgiven on account of it.”⁷⁵ This being the case, rejoice over the affliction of your fast, for by giving your soul to *HaShem* with self-sacrifice, you are certain to affect great things thereby.

footnotes:
⁷⁵ Talmud Bavli, Yoma 86b


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:14)


[צדיקים אומרים מעט ועושים הרבה שהיצה"ר מכביד על האדם ואומר לו שזה הזמן גדול שיענה עצמו והצדיק אומר שירוץ הזמן בזריזות]


The righteous-*Tzaddikim* say little and do much.⁷⁶ For although the evil inclination makes the affliction difficult, by telling him that the suffering will be too long to endure, the *Tzaddik* answers that the time will pass quickly.

footnotes:
⁷⁶ Talmud Bavli, Bava Metziah 87a; Mishnah Avot 1:15


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:15)


וישן בתחילה בג' ימים הראשונים כדי שיתחזקו המוחין אבל לא הרבה מאוד. ישב פעם במקום זה ופעם במקום זה וילך מעט ואח"כ ישכב מעט כדי לבטל צערו. וילמוד במחשבה לבד בלי דיבור כדי להקל צערו מעליו.


The following should be your practice during fast days: Sleep well on the first three nights in order to strengthen your mental faculties. However, do not sleep too much. Change the place where you sit, sometimes seating yourself in one place and sometimes in another place. Go for short walks and lay down for a short while afterwards, to minimize your affliction. When studying Torah during a fast, do so in thought only, without speaking, to minimize your suffering.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:16)


פעמים יש יבשות בפיו ומר מאוד ויראה לו היצר שכואב לו הראש ולא יוכל לסבול. וכשיבטח בחסד הבורא יתחזק כחו ולא יכאוב לו כלל.


At times you may have dryness of mouth accompanied by a very bitter taste, and your evil inclination will tell you that the pain in your head is unbearable. However, if you place your trust in the kindness of the Creator, blessed is He, you will be strengthened and the pain will dissipate.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 43:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/43:17)


ויכוין לעשות נחת רוח להבורא יתברך בתעניתו וכדי להקל צער מהשכינה הוא מקבל עליו צער ויעבוד בשמחה ויכוין שהשכינה תסעדו כמו שהוא סועדת שאר חולים ויעזור לו השי"ת:


Have in mind to give satisfaction of spirit (*Nachat Ruach*) to the Creator, blessed is He, through your fasting and that you are accepting this affliction upon yourself in order to minimize the sorrow of the *Shechinah*. Serve *HaShem* with joy, keep in mind that the *Shechinah-*Divine Presence is sustaining you, and *HaShem*, blessed is He, will assist you.



## Chapter 44

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/44:1)


**לפעמים מטעה היצה"ר לאדם** ואומר לו שעבר עבירה גדולה אע"פ שאינו אלא חומרא בעלמא או שאינה עבירה כלל. וכוונתו שיהא האדם בעצבות ומכח זה. יבוטל בעצבותו מעבודת הבורא ית'.


At times the evil inclination deceives a person, telling him that he transgressed a grave transgression, even though it is merely only a stringency, or not a sin at all. His intention is to bring a person to sadness through it, for as a result of depression he will be unable to serve the Creator, blessed is He.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 44:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/44:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/44:2)


וצריך האדם להבין הרמאות הזה ויאמר להיצה"ר איני משגיח על החומרא שאתה אומר שכוונתך לבטלני מעבודתו יתב' ושקר אתה דובר. וגם אם הוא באמת קצת חטא. יותר יהי' נחת רוח לבוראי שלא אשגיח על החומרא שאתה אומר לי לגרום לי עצבות בעבודתו. אדרבא אעבוד אותו בשמחה. כי זהו כלל גדול כי אין כוונתי בעבודה לצורך עצמי רק לעשות נחת רוח לפניו יתברך וא"כ אף שלא אשגיח על החומרא שאתה אומר לא יקפיד הבורא עלי כי כל עיקר שאיני משגיח הוא מחמת שלא אבטל מעבודתו ית' ואיך אבטל מעבודתו אפילו רגע אחד. וזהו כלל גדול בעבודת הבורא ית' שיזהר מעצבות כל מה שיוכל.


Therefore, understand the duplicity in this, and tell the evil inclination, “I will not pay attention to the stringency you are telling me. You speak falsely and your only intention is to obstruct me from service of *HaShem*, blessed is He. Even if it is in fact a slight transgression, it will certainly give my Creator much greater satisfaction if I pay no heed to what you are saying to depress me in my service of *HaShem*. On the contrary, I will serve Him with joy, because generally, my intention in this service is not for my own sake, but solely to give satisfaction to *HaShem*, blessed is He. This being so, although I am paying no heed to this stringency, my Creator will not treat me severely because of it. After all, the whole reason that I am not paying attention to it, is so that my service of *HaShem*, blessed is He, will not be obstructed, for, how can I be idle from His service for even a moment.”



## Chapter 45

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/45:1)


**הבכי' הוא רעה מאד** שהאדם צריך לעבוד בשמחה רק אם הבכי' הוא מחמת שמחה אז טובה הוא מאד:


Weeping is very bad, for *HaShem* must be served specifically with joy. However, weeping that arises from joy and adhesion to *HaShem*, blessed is He, is very good.



## Chapter 46

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:1)


**ואל ירבה בדקדוקים יתירים בכל דבר שעושה** שזה כוונת היצה"ר לעשות לאדם מורא שמא אינו יוצא בדבר זה כדי להביא אותו לעצבות. ועצבות היא מניעה גדולה לעבודת הבורא ית'.


A person who is just starting out in the service of *HaShem* should not be overly scrupulous and stringent about everything he does. For, the intention of the evil inclination is to make you depressed by causing you to fear that you have not fulfilled your obligations in every detail. However, depression is a terrible and destructive quality that obstructs a person from the service of *HaShem*, blessed is He and must be avoided.⁷⁷

footnotes:
⁷⁷ See Shaarei Kedusha of Rabbi Chaim Vital, 2:4, translated as Gates of Holiness.  Also see Tanya, Igeret HaKodesh, Epistle 11.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 46:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:2)


ואפי' נכשל בעבירה לא ירבה בעצבות שיבוטל העבודה. רק שיעצב על העבירה ויחזור לשמוח בהבורא ית' כיון שהוא מתחרט בחרטה גמורה ובדעתו שלא ישוב לכסלתו בשום פעם.


Even if you actually did stumble and sin, God forbid, do not be overly depressed over it, thereby tearing down everything you have accomplished and giving up on yourself, because this causes you to refrain from progressing in your service of *HaShem,*blessed is He. Instead, be upset and ashamed about the sin before the Creator, beg Him to remove your wickedness, resolve never to revert to this foolishness again and return to rejoicing in *HaShem,* the Creator, blessed is He.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 46:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:3)


ואפילו כשיודע בודאי שאינו יוצא בדבר אחד מחמת מניעות הרבה לא יהי' עצב ויחשוב שהבורא ית' בוחן לבות וכליות שהוא יודע שרצונו לעשות מן המובחר רק שאינו יכול. ויחזק עצמו לשמוח בהבורא ית'.


Even if you know, for certain, that you did not fulfill your obligation in a given matter, due to numerous obstacles, do not be depressed. Consider that *HaShem,*the Creator, blessed is He, “Examines the heart and innards”⁷⁸ and that He knows that you wanted to do your best but were unable. You should thus strengthen yourself and rejoice in the Creator, blessed is He.

footnotes:
⁷⁸ Psalms 7:10


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 46:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:4)


וכמ"ש עת לעשות לד' הפרו תורתך שפעמים יש מצוה שיש בה קצת נדנוד עבירה אל ישגיח על היצה"ר שרוצה לבטלו מלעשות המצוה ויאמר ליצר הלא אין כוונתי באותה המצוה רק אני רוצה לעשות נחת רוח להבורא ית' במצוה זו ובזה יסתלק היצה"ר ממנו בעז"ה אבל מ"מ צריך להבחין בשכלו אם יעשה המצוה הזאת אם לא.


The verse states,⁷⁹ “It is a time to do for *HaShem*, they have abrogated Your Torah.” It is explained⁸⁰ that there are times in which the fulfillment of a *mitzvah* may contain an admixture of sin, but that, nevertheless, one must pay no heed to the evil inclination who wishes to prevent him from fulfilling it. Under such circumstances, tell the evil inclination: “In fulfilling this commandment, my intention was certainly not to anger the Creator, blessed is He, nor was it for my own honor. Had I known that the Creator does not desire that I do this, I would certainly not have done it. My only desire is to give satisfaction of spirit to *HaShem*, the Creator, blessed is He, through the fulfillment of this *mitzvah*.” With this, the evil inclination will withdraw from you, with *HaShem’s*help. Nonetheless, before doing something, use your intellect to discern whether or not you should do it.

footnotes:
⁷⁹ Psalms 119:126
⁸⁰ Talmud Bavli, Brachot 63a


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 46:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/46:5)


וכל מה שכתבתי הם כללים גדולים ונחמדים מפז רב כל דבר ודבר הוא כלל גדול:


All the above are very great and important principles that are, “More desirable than fine gold.”⁸¹ Each of them is an important principle.

footnotes:
⁸¹ Psalms 19:11



## Chapter 47

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/47:1)


**איתא הרבה עשו כר"ש ב"י** ולא עלתה בידם הכוונה היא שהי' רוצים לעשות לעצמם סיגופים כדי שיבואו למדרגת רשב"י ולכך לא עלתה בידם. ולא יכוין בעבודתו רק לעשות נחת רוח להבורא ית' לבד ולא שיבא למדרגה.


It states,⁸² “Many have acted like Rabbi Shimon bar Yochai, but were not successful.” The intention is that they wanted to undergo self-mortifications in order to attain the level of Rabbi Shimon bar Yochai (Rashbi). This is why they were unsuccessful. One’s intention in the service of *HaShem* should solely be to give satisfaction to the Creator, and nothing else. That is, your intention should not be to attain a lofty level of spiritual standing.

footnotes:
⁸² Talmud Bavli, Brachot 35b



## Chapter 48

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 48:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/48:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/48:1)


כשהוא רואה שעבודתו גדולה מחבירו לא יתגאה כדאיתא. ואל יאמר אני גדול מחבירי.


If a person sees that his own service of *HaShem* is greater than others, he should not be arrogant, God forbid. This is as stated in *Otiyot d’Rabbi Akiva*,⁸³ “One should not say in his heart, I am greater than my fellow.”

footnotes:
⁸³ Otiyot d’Rabbi Akiva, Letter Dalet (pg. 20)



## Chapter 49

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 49:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/49:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/49:1)


ועוד כלל גדול כשיחרפוהו בני אדם על עבודתו בתפלה או בשאר דברים לא יענה להם אפי' בדברים טובים כדי שלא יבא לידי מחלוקת ולידי גבהות משכחות הבורא ית'. וארז"ל שתיקתו של אדם יפה מדיבורו שהוא מביאתו לידי ענוה.


Another important principle: If people mock you over your service in prayer or other matters, do not respond at all.⁸⁴ Do not even respond with positive words, so as not to be drawn into arguments or pride, which cause a person to forget the Creator, blessed is He. Our sages of blessed memory stated,⁸⁵ “A person’s silence leads to humility.”

footnotes:
⁸⁴ Shulach Aruch, Orach Chayim 1:1; Mishneh Torah, Hilchot De’ot 2:3
⁸⁵ Shaarei Kedusha of Rabbi Chaim Vital, 2:5, translated as Gates of Holiness.



## Chapter 50

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 50:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/50:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/50:1)


וגם לא יסתכל בפני ב"א אפילו כשמדבר עמהם אם מחשבותיהם אינם לבורא ית' תמיד דבוקה כי יהי' לו פגם בנפשו מחמת הסתכלותו.


When speaking with others, do not look at the face of anyone whose thoughts are not always bonded to *HaShem,* the Creator, blessed is he, for doing so can blemish the soul.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 50:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/50:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/50:2)


ובבני אדם כשירים שמחשבותיהם דבוקה בהבורא ית' יסתכל דוקא ויקנה קדושה בנפשו:


However, regarding the faces of those whose thoughts are always in a state of adhesion to *HaShem,* the Creator, blessed is He, make a point of looking at them and acquire holiness in your soul.



## Chapter 51

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 51:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/51:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/51:1)


**הלימוד יהי' בכח ובשמחה** גדולה וזהו ממעט מחשבות זרות.


Your study of Torah should always be with strength and great joy. This helps diminish wrong thoughts.



## Chapter 52

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 52:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/52:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/52:1)


כשאדם עובד תמיד אותו ית' בכל רגע אין לו פנאי להתגאות ולאהוב את הגאוה ושאר מדות הרעות:


When one serves *HaShem*, blessed is He, constantly, at all times, he has no free time to be prideful or to love pride, or any other bad character traits.



## Chapter 53

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 53:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/53:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/53:1)


**ישים עצמו כמי שאינו** כדאיתא בגמרא על פסוק והחכמה מאין תמצא. והכוונה שיחשוב כמו שאינו בעוה"ז מה תועלת יהי' שאהי' חשוב בעיני ב"א.


One should consider himself as if he does not exist, as it states in the Talmud⁸⁶ regarding the verse,⁸⁷ “Wisdom is found from nothingness.” The intention is that one should consider himself as if he is not in this world, in which case it is of no consequence to him whether he is important in the eyes of others or not.

footnotes:
⁸⁶ Talmud Bavli, Sota 21b
⁸⁷ Job 28:12



## Chapter 54

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 54:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/54:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/54:1)


כשלומד צריך ליישב עצמו לפני מי הוא לומד שלפעמים מתרחק עצמו בלימודו מהבורא ית' לכך הוא צריך ליישב עצמו בכל עת ובכל שעה:


When studying Torah, center yourself and realize before Whom you study. For at times, one’s studies can distance his mind from *HaShem,* the Creator, blessed is He. It therefore is necessary to re-center yourself at regular intervals, every hour or so.



## Chapter 55

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 55:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/55:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/55:1)


**אם חושק לאיזה מצוה** יראה וישתדל לעשותה ואל יבטל אותו היצה"ר לומר שהוא דבר גדול שיוכל לבא לידי יוהרא אעפ"כ לא יבטלנו. אך יזהר מאד אף שיבא התפארות באמצע המעשה ירחק אותו בחוזק ובהתלהבות ובודאי לבסוף יעשה לשמה ממש בלא שום התפארות שמשלא לשמה יבא לידי לשמה.


If you have an impulse to do a particular *mitzvah*, see to making every effort to do so. Do not allow the evil inclination to dissuade you, by telling you that this is something exceedingly great, through which you will come to haughtiness. Even so, you should still do it and not be obstructed. However, be very cautious. Even if you are roused with pride while performing the good deed, push it away with great strength and passion and, ultimately, you will certainly do it for the sake of *HaShem* alone, without any self-aggrandizement. As our sages said,⁸⁸ “One should always engage in Torah and *mitzvot*, even if not for the sake of *HaShem*. For, through fulfilling them not for the sake of *HaShem*, he will come to fulfill them for the sake of *HaShem*.”

footnotes:
⁸⁸ Talmud Bavli, Pesachim 50b


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 55:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/55:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/55:2)


וירבה במצות כל מה שיוכל והקב"ה יעזור לו שיהי' הכל בלי פני' אך יתחזק עצמו בכל יכולתו:


Do as many *mitzvot* as you can, and *HaShem*, blessed is He, will assist you that it will be without self-interest. Nevertheless, you too must strengthen yourself in this matter, as much as possible.



## Chapter 56

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 56:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/56:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/56:1)


**כשאדם יש לו חשק להתענות** אז צריך ליזהר שלא לבטל מאותו דבר שהוא רוצה לעשות אע"פ שיודע שטוב יותר לעבוד השי"ת בשמחה בלי סיגופים כי הם גורמים עצבות. אבל מסתמא ידע אינש בנפשי' שצריך להתענות שעדיין לא תיקן נפשו כראוי.


When a person has an impulse to take on a fast of abstention, he must be careful not to be obstructed from fulfilling it, even though he knows it is better to serve *HaShem*, blessed is He, with joy and without self-affliction, which can lead to depression. Nevertheless, we assume that a person knows himself and is aware in himself that he needs to undergo a fast of abstention, because his soul has not been rectified, as fitting.


###### Tzava'at HaRivash 56:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/56:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/56:2)


וכן יש כמה וכמה דברים שזה צריך לדקדק מצד נשמתו ולהחמיר על עצמו חומרות וזה אין צריך לדקדק כל כך.


Likewise, there may be any number of things that he should be scrupulous about, such as taking on certain stringencies, because he knows his own nature, whereas others may not need to be as scrupulous regarding the same matters.



## Chapter 57

###### Tzava'at HaRivash 57:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/57:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Tzava%27at%20HaRivash/r/57:1)
